| B-309 | P2 | Verified | User asked that Client Management get "a dedicated email address that will automatically fetch the email so that we can give a better service," with a dedicated admin configuration page, explicitly requesting research into how leading tools (Zendesk, Freshdesk, Help Scout, Intercom, Zoho Desk) do this before implementing. Chose, via explicit clarifying questions, full two-way email (inbound + outbound, not inbound-only -- confirmed no real vendor ships inbound-only as a supported mode) and real-mailbox verification (a disposable GreenMail Docker IMAP+SMTP server) over mocks. New `ClientMailbox` model (tenant-scoped, admin-managed, modeled as a list mirroring `IntegrationConnection`'s per-row shape rather than the global `PlatformSetting` mechanism, which was confirmed too single-row/global for this) holds IMAP/SMTP connection settings with passwords encrypted via the same `settings_cipher()`-backed property pattern `IntegrationConnection.secret` already uses (migration `20260811_0073`). A new `serviceops_core/email_ingest.py` (pure parsing/threading, zero I/O dependency, mirrors `client_automation.py`'s "engine has no I/O" shape) implements the researched conventions: Message-ID/In-Reply-To/References headers are the primary threading signal, a bracketed `[CXTnnnnnnn]` subject token is the documented fallback (per Zendesk's own docs, since subject tags get stripped/reordered by clients/gateways); a sender's `ClientContact` is always auto-created, `ClientOrganization` auto-linking by sender domain is gated by a hardcoded free-mail denylist (gmail/yahoo/outlook/hotmail/icloud/aol/protonmail/live/msn) matching Freshdesk's documented "blacklist free-mail domains from company auto-linking" convention, falling back to the mailbox's configured default organization; `Auto-Submitted`/`Precedence` headers reject autoresponders/bulk mail before ticket creation, and the app's own outbound replies are stamped `Auto-Submitted: no`; per-sender inbound rate limiting reuses the existing `route_rate_limit()` helper as a last-resort circuit breaker (mirrors Zendesk's documented per-sender hourly ceiling). New `process_client_email_inbox()` (app.py, wired into `tools/outbox_worker.py`'s periodic loop like every other background job) polls each active mailbox via `imaplib`, marks each message `\Seen` immediately after fetch (before processing) so a downstream exception can't cause reprocessing, and funnels ticket creation through the same `attach_slas()`/`evaluate_client_triggers()` calls manual creation already uses. `deliver_client_email_reply()` sends an agent's public reply back out via the mailbox's SMTP config with correct `In-Reply-To`/References threading. `ClientTicketMessage.author_id` was made nullable (an inbound-email message has no internal `User` author -- rendered as "<contact> (via email)"); `ClientTicketMessage` gained `message_id`/`in_reply_to`; `FileAttachment` gained a nullable `client_ticket_id`, and the malware-scan/hash/storage tail of `save_ticket_attachment()` (which had no actual Flask-`FileStorage` dependency) was extracted into a parallel `save_email_attachment()` for raw email-attachment bytes, deliberately not a refactor of the original upload path. New admin page `/client-management/mailboxes` (`templates/client_mailboxes_admin.html`, mirrors `client_macros_admin.html`'s list+toggle+add-form shape) linked from Client Management's admin toolbar. **Real GreenMail (Docker) end-to-end verification** (not mocks) against the live deployed stack, all 4 tests passing: (1) a real inbound email created a ticket with correctly auto-created contact/organization; (2) a real agent reply was delivered via real SMTP with correct `In-Reply-To`/subject-token threading, confirmed by connecting to the customer's own real IMAP inbox; (3) a second reply referencing the agent's own reply's Message-ID threaded into the same ticket, not a duplicate; (4) an `Auto-Submitted: auto-replied` email was correctly rejected, no ticket created. That same verification pass caught a real bug before ship: the reply-send path picked *any* active `ClientMailbox` for the tenant rather than the one the ticket actually came in on, and silently sent nothing (no flash, no log) when no mailbox happened to be active -- fixed by adding `ClientTicket.mailbox_id` (migration `20260811_0074`) to remember the originating mailbox and preferring it, falling back to the tenant's sole active mailbox only for manually-created tickets, with an explicit flashed warning when no active mailbox exists at all. Disclosed scope limitations, not attempted this pass: basic IMAP/SMTP auth only, no OAuth (Gmail/O365 require an app password, not the normal account password, under basic auth); no content-based spam scoring beyond the loop/rate-limit defenses above; the free-mail denylist is hardcoded, not admin-configurable; the reply-mailbox fallback for manually-created tickets assumes a single mailbox per tenant even though the model supports several. Version bumped to `1.59.0` (minor: new user-facing capability). Evidence: full suite `424 passed, 2 skipped` (new coverage: `tests/test_email_ingest.py` -- 9 unit tests for parsing/threading/free-mail/auto-generated detection, no I/O; `tests/test_app.py` -- mailbox admin tenant-isolation/admin-only tests, inbound-ticket-creation-with-auto-org test, threaded-reply-does-not-duplicate test, auto-generated-mail-rejected test, outbound-SMTP-delivery-with-Message-ID-storage test, using a fake IMAP connection test double for the app-level tests). Migrations `20260811_0073`/`20260811_0074` each verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against real Postgres 16. Deployed live (`serviceops-app:1.59.0`) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head `20260811_0074` confirmed both via `/ready` JSON and a direct `alembic_version` query. Closing disclosure given to the user: this environment cannot verify against their actual production mailbox -- after deploy, they must configure a real mailbox on `/client-management/mailboxes` and send one real test email themselves to confirm true end-to-end delivery in their own environment. |
| B-308 | P2 | Verified | Phases 2-7 (the remainder) of the Client Management build-out started in [[B-307]], delivered in one continuous pass per explicit user instruction ("complete phase 2 to 7 continuously"), each phase still independently built/tested/migrated/deployed/live-verified before moving to the next, then shipped together in a single commit/tag/release since intermediate versions were never separately committed to git during the run. **Phase 2, custom fields**: tenant-wide `ClientCustomFieldDefinition` schema (text/number/date/select) for tickets/organizations/contacts -- field *existence* is tenant-wide (matches Zendesk's own model), with per-organization required/visible overrides stored in the org's own `settings` JSON (a new column, also the foundation phase 7 reuses) rather than a second table; values stored in a new `custom_fields` JSON column on each of the three entities (migration `20260810_0068`). **Phase 3, saved Views**: new `ClientView` model (migration `20260811_0069`) sitting on top of the *existing* filter-condition engine (`parse_list_filter_param`/`apply_filter_conditions`, already used by CMDB/ticket lists) rather than inventing bespoke filtering -- the customer ticket list's filter bar now reuses the exact same `.list-filter` JS component the Incidents/Changes lists already use. Views can be private or shared tenant-wide; the six built-in views (mine/unassigned/pending/recent/solved/unsolved) stay hardcoded and unchanged. **Phase 4, Macros**: new `ClientMacro` model (migration `20260811_0070`) -- one-click bulk field changes plus an optional canned reply, applied via a new dropdown on the ticket detail page, only rendered when at least one active macro exists. **Phase 5, per-organization SLA policies**: `SLADefinition` gains a nullable `client_organization_id` (migration `20260811_0071`, same "null = tenant-wide default" convention used everywhere else in this app) -- `attach_slas()` now prefers an org-specific row over the tenant default for the same priority when both exist; `sync_slas()` extended to recognize client tickets' own "Solved" terminal state (ITIL tickets never use it, so this was previously dead for customer tickets -- their SLA clock would never stop on resolution, only on the later "Closed" transition). **Phase 6, Triggers**: new `serviceops_core/client_automation.py` -- a small, closed-vocabulary condition→action engine (deliberately single-condition/single-action, not a multi-row rule builder) scoped to `ClientTicket`'s own fields, since the existing `serviceops_core/workflow.py` engine is ITIL-ticket-specific by design and not a clean fit; new `ClientTrigger` model (migration `20260811_0072`) evaluated on ticket create/update/status-change. **Phase 7, branding + escalation policy**: per-organization display name/accent color (reuses phase 2's `settings` column, no new migration) shown on that organization's own tickets -- a logo-image field was deliberately *not* built despite being in the original plan, since this app's CSP (`img-src 'self' data:`) would silently block any externally-hosted logo and there's no upload/storage path for one; shipping a control that silently doesn't work was rejected in favor of disclosing the gap. A new `escalation_hours`/`escalation_group_id` policy per organization is enforced by a new periodic `process_client_escalation_policies()` job wired into the same background-worker loop as SLA-breach detection -- idempotent via an `auto-escalated` tag (no dedicated boolean column was added for one flag).

**Real bug found and fixed during this same pass, not a separate incident**: while live-verifying the new Saved-View delete button, its confirmation dialog never appeared -- traced to an inline `onsubmit="return confirm(...)"` attribute, which this app's own CSP (`script-src 'self'`, no `unsafe-inline`) silently blocks, so the delete submitted immediately with zero prompt. A repo-wide grep found the same broken pattern on **eight** pre-existing forms this session didn't touch (reset role to baseline, erase user, clear error log, clear all notifications, delete rack, delete change, revoke session), plus a related `onchange="this.form.submit()"` auto-submit filter on the Improvements page silently not working for the same CSP reason. Fixed centrally via a delegated `data-confirm`/`data-auto-submit` listener pair in `static/platform.js` (mirroring the established fix for an identical print-button CSP issue earlier in this app's history) -- every affected template converted to the data attribute, a duplicate confirm-on-submit implementation in `static/discovery.js` removed now that it's redundant, and a new repo-wide regression test (`test_no_template_uses_an_inline_event_handler_blocked_by_csp`) scans every shipped template so a future inline handler is caught in CI rather than only by a live browser check.

Version bumped to `1.58.0` (minor: six new capabilities in one release). Evidence: full suite `410 passed, 2 skipped` (up from `383/2` before this batch) -- new tests per phase (required-field enforcement + per-org overrides for custom fields; a full create→filter→save→share→delete cycle across three real sessions for Views; field-changes-plus-canned-reply and deactivation for Macros; org-override-beats-tenant-default plus the Solved-state SLA-completion fix for phase 5; condition-matches/skips plus deactivation for Triggers, plus 20 isolated unit tests for the new `client_automation` module's operator semantics and fail-closed behavior on unknown fields/ops; branding persistence plus a full escalate→tag→note→notify→idempotent-second-run cycle for phase 7) and the new CSP-inline-handler regression scanner. Each of the five new migrations (`20260810_0068` through `20260811_0072`) was individually verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against real Postgres 16 before that phase shipped. Deployed live and progressively verified at each phase's own version (`serviceops-app:1.53.0` through `1.58.0`) -- `/health`/`/ready` green throughout, final migration head `20260811_0072`. Verified end-to-end with real Playwright/Chromium sessions per phase against the live app, not just the backend: custom field dropdown/required-field/per-org-override behavior on real forms; a real filter→save→share→delete cycle for Views including confirming the CSP fix's dialog actually appears and Cancel actually blocks the delete; a real macro application changing a real ticket's priority and posting a real internal-note reply; the SLA target-type dropdown's client-side show/hide toggle for the organization-override field, and a created SLA definition showing the org name in the table; a real trigger firing on a matching ticket and not firing on a non-matching one, and not firing at all once deactivated; a real backdated ticket actually escalated by the live background worker after its threshold passed, with the correct internal note, tag, and (where the team had a manager) notification. Not done this pass, disclosed rather than silently skipped: the logo-image branding field (CSP-blocked, see above); a `v1.50.1` git tag that fell outside the last-5/`.0` retention window was flagged for pruning but the deletion was blocked by this session's own safety guardrails against destructive git operations -- left in place for the user to remove manually rather than forcing it. |
| B-307 | P2 | Verified | Phase 1 of a larger, user-requested Client Management build-out toward Zendesk-style agent workflow (saved Views, Macros, per-org SLA policies, Triggers) plus deep admin configuration (custom fields, per-org access control, branding, notification/escalation policy) -- scoped via clarifying questions into 7 phases, delivered independently with full verification per phase rather than one unverified mega-batch. This entry covers phase 1: per-organization visibility. Previously, Client Management access was strictly all-or-nothing -- `user_can_access_client_management` (admin or SysOps group member) meant any qualifying user saw every `ClientOrganization`/`ClientTicket`/`ClientContact` in the tenant, with no way to restrict a sensitive customer account to specific agents/teams. `ClientOrganization` gains `restricted_visibility` (Boolean, default False -- **opt-in**, so every existing/new org keeps exactly today's behavior until an admin explicitly restricts it, zero behavior change for anyone who never uses the feature) and a new `ClientOrganizationAccess` grant table (`(tenant_id, organization_id, user_id OR group_id)`, mirroring `CiClassPermission`/`RolePolicyOverride`'s established sparse-override idiom -- migration `20260810_0067`). A new organization detail page (`/client-management/organizations/<id>`, `templates/client_organization_detail.html`) lets an admin toggle restriction and add/remove per-user or per-team access grants; the organizations list links each name to it and flags restricted orgs with a badge. New `visible_client_organization_query`/`visible_client_ticket_query`/`visible_client_contact_query` helpers (`app.py`) replace every flat `tenant_query(Client*)` call site across the module -- ticket/org/contact lists, dashboard counts, ticket creation's contact picker, and (found and fixed during this same pass, not a separate bug) the global `/ui/search` results, which would otherwise have kept leaking restricted organizations/tickets/contacts to search even after an admin restricted them. No route was missed: every Client Management query site was audited and converted, not just the obviously user-facing ones. Version bumped to `1.52.0` (minor: new capability). Evidence: full suite `383 passed, 2 skipped` (up from `380/2`) -- new tests cover the opt-in default (existing/new orgs stay open), a full restrict/grant/block/revoke cycle across three real user sessions (granted agent sees it, blocked agent gets 404 on the detail page and no result from either the org list or `/ui/search`, admin-only enforcement on the toggle/grant/revoke actions themselves returning 403 for a non-admin manager). Migration `20260810_0067` verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against real Postgres 16, confirming both the "already exists via baseline metadata.create_all" path and the actual add-column/create-table path leave the correct column default, table, FKs (5), and indexes in place. Deployed live (`serviceops-app:1.52.0`) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head `20260810_0067`. Verified end-to-end with a real Playwright/Chromium session against the live app: toggled restriction via the real button, granted access via the real select-and-submit form, confirmed a granted SysOps member sees the organization (200) while a blocked SysOps member does not (404, absent from the org list, absent from `/ui/search` results specifically -- not just absent from the page's raw text, which would have been a false pass since the search box echoes the query string itself), removed the grant via the real UI and confirmed the previously-granted user also lost access; zero unexpected browser console errors across every step. Not done this pass, explicitly the next phase per the plan: saved Views (phase 3, the user's own top pick), Macros, per-org SLA policies, Triggers, custom fields, branding, and notification/escalation policy all remain to build. |
| B-306 | P2 | Verified | Two small reported issues, fixed together. (1) User asked whether a "Conflict detection completed / No conflict" ticket-timeline entry needs to show at all -- it appeared on every single change regardless of outcome, purely routine noise. Root-caused to `run_change_conflict_detection()` (`app.py`) logging unconditionally; the app's own established convention elsewhere (the SLA-breach scan, for one) only logs the exceptional/actionable outcome, staying silent on the boring/expected result. Brought this function in line: the ticket-facing history entry now only writes when a conflict is actually found; the tamper-evident audit log (a separate call, unaffected) still always records the check either way, so nothing is lost for compliance purposes -- just not shown to the user as clutter. (2) User asked that "all the options... must be searchable... including even a little sub item," giving "Security and limits" as an example of something unfindable. Root-caused: `/ui/search`'s hardcoded `navigation` list (`app.py`) only had one entry per top-level admin page (e.g. "Platform settings"), zero entries for the ~30 anchored sub-sections inside those pages (settings groups, governance sub-panels, health-page sections) -- direct `#anchor` links to them already existed and worked, they just weren't indexed for search. Added an entry per sub-section across Platform settings (generated from the existing `SETTING_GROUP_META` dict plus two static ones, so it stays in sync automatically as settings groups are added/renamed), Service delivery and governance (13 hand-listed anchors), and System health (4). No schema/migration change. Version bumped to `1.51.2` (patch). Evidence: full suite `380 passed, 2 skipped` (up from `379/2`) -- new regression tests confirm a no-conflict change creation leaves zero "Conflict detection completed" history rows while `conflict_status` still correctly records "No conflict" internally, and that searching "security"/"freeze windows" now returns the right settings sub-sections with a working `#anchor` URL. Deployed live (`serviceops-app:1.51.2`) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head unchanged. Verified end-to-end against the live app: a real authenticated `/ui/search?q=security` JSON request returned "Security and limits" with `.../admin/settings#settings-security` in its URL. Not done this pass, flagged as a real gap rather than silently claimed complete: `admin_access.html`/`integrations_admin.html`/`workflows_admin.html`/`admin_roles.html` were checked and found to have no anchored sub-sections today (single-scroll pages), so nothing further to index there yet, but any sub-navigation added to those pages in the future needs to be mirrored into this same hand-maintained list -- there is no automatic derivation from the Jinja templates. |
| B-305 | P0 | Verified | User hit a real, reproducible bug from live use: set a change freeze window, tried to schedule a Change inside it (correctly rejected), then -- without reloading the page -- edited the date outside the freeze and resubmitted from the same form, and got "The security token is missing or expired. Refresh the page and try again," forcing a full restart of the form. User noted seeing this "in many places," not just this one form. Root-caused to `inject_csrf` (`app.py`): the after-request hook that embeds the hidden `_csrf_token` field into every rendered form was gated on `response.status_code < 400`. `ticket_new`'s `render_form(error)` (used by both incident and change creation) correctly returns `400` on a validation failure -- including the freeze-window rejection -- so that re-rendered error page shipped with **no CSRF token embedded at all**. The user's very next submit from that same page had nothing to send, and failed CSRF validation even though their session had not changed in any way -- the token itself was never stale, the page simply never got one. Since `inject_csrf` is the single, centralized place every form's hidden token comes from (no template embeds it directly), this silently affected **every** form in the app that re-renders itself with a 4xx on validation failure -- exactly matching the user's "many places" observation, not just the change-freeze case. Fixed by removing the status-code gate entirely (`mimetype == "text/html"` is sufficient; 3xx redirects have no body to inject into and a fresh GET follows regardless, so they were never actually protected by that condition in the first place). Also addressed the user's second, smaller ask ("show change freeze nicely somewhere, including the calendar"): this app has no calendar view of any kind yet, so building one was treated as out of proportion for this fix -- instead, added a new amber `.flash.warning` banner (new CSS, `static/app.css`) to both the Change creation form (`templates/ticket_form.html`) and a Change ticket's governance section (`templates/ticket_detail.html`), listing every active/upcoming freeze window by name, date range, and reason, so a user sees the block coming instead of only discovering it after a failed submit. A dedicated calendar view remains a real possible follow-up if wanted, not attempted here. No schema/migration change. Version bumped to `1.51.1` (patch: bug fix + a small UX addition alongside it). Evidence: full suite `379 passed, 2 skipped` (up from `378/2`) -- new regression test `test_csrf_token_survives_a_validation_error_resubmit_on_the_same_page` reproduces the exact bug shape (a 400 validation response, asserts a `_csrf_token` field is present in it, then resubmits using exactly that token and asserts success) against a real CSRF-enabled app instance, the same pattern the existing `test_csrf_protects_login_and_authenticated_mutations` already established. Deployed live (`serviceops-app:1.51.1`) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head unchanged at `20260810_0066`. Verified end-to-end with a real Playwright/Chromium session reproducing the user's exact reported sequence against the live app: created a real freeze window, loaded "New Change" and confirmed the new banner rendered *before* any submission listing the active window(s); submitted with dates inside the freeze and got the expected `400` with the exact validation message, form re-rendered with entered data preserved and (confirmed) a `_csrf_token` hidden input now present; without reloading, edited only the dates to fall outside the freeze and resubmitted from the same page -- succeeded, redirected to the new change's detail page, zero CSRF error text anywhere in the response; zero unexpected browser console errors throughout. Incidentally surfaced (not fixed, out of scope for this entry): a `409` on Normal-change creation ("Executive approval authority must be configured") on this fresh instance -- a CCB-governance configuration gap, unrelated to CSRF, noted for a future entry. |
| B-304 | P1 | Verified | User asked for a self-review of the whole project against CLAUDE.md's "Current review priorities" and to fix items #4, #5, #9, #13, #15 plus whatever else the review turned up, shipping only after deep verification. A fresh audit (3 parallel `Explore` passes) found 10 of the 15 priorities already closed with evidence from prior work, and pinned down concrete root causes for the 5 that weren't: (4) `Comment`/`GroupMember`/`CatalogItemRouting`/`RequestedItem`/`CatalogTask`/`FileAttachment`/the legacy `Approval` model had no `tenant_id` of their own, relying entirely on every query site remembering to join back to a tenant-owning parent; (5) no memory ceiling existed on the app container at all (an unbounded query/leak would grow until the *host's* OOM-killer intervened) plus a few genuinely uncapped queries; (9) `deliver_webhook`'s DNS-rebinding validation (`integration_endpoint_resolves_safely`) re-resolved and checked a hostname immediately before delivery, but `requests.post()` then performed its own independent resolution to actually connect -- a second, unvalidated DNS answer in the gap between the two; (13) `app.py` (12,685 lines) is still growing faster than `serviceops_core/` is absorbing it; (15) Playwright has never been a tracked dependency in this repo -- every live-browser check this project has ever done (see [[B-297]], [[B-303]]) was an ephemeral install inside a throwaway verification context, zero standing regression coverage.

**#4 -- tenant_id gaps, migration `20260810_0066`.** Added `tenant_id` to all seven models, matching `CiClassPermission`/`RolePolicyOverride`'s existing column template; backfilled via `UPDATE ... SET tenant_id = (SELECT tenant_id FROM <parent> WHERE ...)` per table (`RequestedItem` backfilled before `CatalogTask` since the latter's backfill reads the former), then `NOT NULL` + FK + index, guarded and skipped on SQLite the same way `20260807_0064`/`20260807_0065` already established. Every in-app construction site for these 7 models was audited and given an explicit `tenant_id=<parent>.tenant_id` instead of relying on the ambient `tenant_context_id()` default (which silently resolves to tenant 1 outside a request context -- correct for the common case, but explicit is safer at every site touched here). Also fixed a real, pre-existing bug found in `seed_itil()` while auditing this: a second tenant's `seed_itil()` run looped over `CatalogItem.query.all()` with **no tenant filter at all**, meaning it could create a `CatalogItemRouting` row pointing tenant 2's Windows team at tenant 1's catalog item.

The bigger find while hardening call sites: `user_can_manage_ticket`, `user_in_group`, `user_can_manage_ritm`, and `user_can_manage_enterprise_record` each had an **unconditional** `if user.role == "admin": return True` with no tenant check at all -- a tenant-2 admin could manage tenant-1 tickets, group-gated actions (including catalog task updates), RITMs, and enterprise records purely by guessing IDs, since the "admin bypasses membership" shortcut never verified the target record's tenant matched the admin's own. All four now check `record.tenant_id != user.tenant_id` (or the equivalent for `user_in_group`'s `group.tenant_id`) before the role-based bypass. This is a materially more serious finding than the missing columns that prompted the audit -- tracked here rather than as a separate entry since it was found and fixed in the same pass, by the same root-cause investigation.

**#9 -- webhook DNS TOCTOU, `serviceops_core/dns_pin.py`.** New thread-local `socket.getaddrinfo` wrapper: `deliver_webhook()` captures the exact resolved addresses `integration_endpoint_resolves_safely()` (refactored into `resolve_endpoint_addresses_safely()`, now returning the raw `getaddrinfo` result alongside the boolean) already validated, then pins them for the calling thread for the exact duration of the one `requests.post()` call that follows -- `requests`' own internal DNS lookup returns the pinned answer instead of re-resolving, closing the window a short-TTL DNS record could otherwise race. `threading.local()` (not a bare module-level override) because gunicorn serves this app with multiple threads per worker (`tools/gunicorn-entrypoint.sh --threads`) and concurrent webhook deliveries are expected -- verified two threads pinning different hosts concurrently never see each other's pin.

**A real bug was found and fixed during live verification, not before shipping.** The first cut of `_patched_getaddrinfo` returned the pinned address list verbatim regardless of what family/type/proto/port the caller actually asked for. The pin was captured via a bare `getaddrinfo(hostname, None)` -- no socket type requested -- which returns one entry per (address, SOCK_STREAM/SOCK_DGRAM/SOCK_RAW) combination, all with **port 0**. `requests`/`urllib3` then asks for `SOCK_STREAM` specifically and a real port (443); handing back the unfiltered pin meant the caller could get a `SOCK_DGRAM`/`SOCK_RAW` entry with port 0 instead, and every real webhook delivery to a hostname failed with `[Errno 93] Protocol not supported`. Caught by a live-verification subagent that actually exercised `deliver_webhook()` against a real endpoint rather than just checking `/admin/integrations` rendered -- confirmed live, reproduced with a direct `docker exec` call showing the `ConnectionError`. Fixed by filtering pinned candidates to the caller's requested family/type/proto and substituting the caller's real port into each matching entry (falling back to the full pinned set, port-substituted, only if literally nothing matches -- still safe, since every pinned address was already validated, just a worse-than-ideal type match). Added a regression test (`test_pin_filters_to_requested_socket_type_and_substitutes_the_real_port`) that pins a realistic three-socket-type/port-0 tuple set and asserts a `SOCK_STREAM`/port-443 request gets back exactly one correctly-ported `SOCK_STREAM` entry -- this is the test that would have caught the bug before it ever reached live verification, and didn't exist in the first cut. Re-verified live after the fix: a real `deliver_webhook()` call to `https://postman-echo.com/post` (a real external HTTPS endpoint, resolved by hostname, not a literal IP) returned `200` end-to-end through the pinned path.

**#5 -- OOM candidates + memory ceiling.** `cmdb_export` and `analytics_overdue` gained the same `export_limit`/row-cap-plus-truncation-notice pattern `open_work_export` already established (`tickets_export`/`requests_export` already had it; `manager_portal_export` is bounded by headcount, not record volume, so left alone; the CMDB CSV *import*'s `upload.read()` turned out to already be bounded by the existing app-wide `MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH` config, so needed no change -- checked before assuming, per this repo's own discipline). `compose.yaml`'s `app`/`worker` services get a new `mem_limit` (`${APP_MEM_LIMIT:-1g}` / `${WORKER_MEM_LIMIT:-512m}`) -- previously no ceiling existed at all, so an unbounded leak would grow until the *host's* OOM-killer intervened rather than Compose restarting just the one container. Disclosed explicitly, not silently implied as "root-caused": this closes the concrete unbounded-query causes found by static review, not necessarily every possible cause -- real profiling under production load still needs real load-test infrastructure this environment doesn't have.

**#13 -- app.py decomposition, one bounded slice.** `/analytics/overdue`'s computation moved into `serviceops_core/analytics.py` (`overdue_enterprise_records()`), following the existing `workflow.py`/`ci_class_policy.py` pattern (pure data computation, no route/template/`current_user` access, explicit params instead of importing app.py globals). The sibling `/analytics` route (~260 lines, deeply interlinked aggregate queries) was deliberately **not** mechanically relocated in the same pass -- real risk of a subtle behavior change with no automated way to diff a move that size, for a code-quality item lower priority than the security fixes in this same batch. Flagged here as the next candidate slice, not silently dropped.

**#15 -- first persisted browser-regression coverage.** New `requirements-e2e.txt` (Playwright, kept out of `requirements.txt`/the production image the same way `reportlab` is siloed to docs tooling) and `tests/test_e2e_smoke.py` -- skipped by default (`E2E_BASE_URL` env var gate), not part of the normal suite/CI gate, but a real tracked file instead of an ephemeral subagent install. Covers login + dashboard redirect + `/cmdb` navigation + zero console errors. Explicitly a first standing thread, not closure of #15 -- load, failover, accessibility, LDAP, Keycloak, SMTP, Teams, and Kubernetes evidence all still need real staging infrastructure this repo's test suite cannot provide.

**Explicitly out of scope, not silently dropped:** a full `tests/test_app.py` split into per-domain files (197 of ~372 tests live in one file, mirroring app.py's own monolith problem) -- cosmetic reorganization, not a bug, deferred as low-urgency/high-effort. ~30 BACKLOG rows whose stated remaining gap is external infrastructure this environment cannot provide (P0 penetration test, vault rotation ceremony, live Kubernetes failure injection, independent WCAG audit, live LDAP/Keycloak provider evidence) -- already honestly disclosed in their own rows, not re-litigated here.

Version bumped to `1.51.0` (minor: new `tenant_id` columns + new capability, not just fixes). Evidence: full suite `378 passed, 2 skipped` (up from `372/1`; the one new skip is the e2e smoke test, gated off by design). Migration `20260810_0066` verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against real Postgres 16 -- confirming both the "already exists via baseline `metadata.create_all`" path (fresh install) and the actual `add_column`+backfill+`NOT NULL`+FK+index path (a DB predating this change) both leave all 7 tables correctly shaped, with the explicit `fk_<table>_tenant_id`/`ix_<table>_tenant_id` names from the migration itself confirmed present after the real add-column path. Deployed live to the real Compose stack (`serviceops-app:1.51.0`, not a throwaway) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head `20260810_0066` confirmed both via `/ready` JSON and a direct `alembic_version` query. Verified end-to-end against the live deployment: created a real second tenant + tenant-2 admin, a real tenant-1 support group/RITM/catalog task, and confirmed the tenant-2 admin gets `403` (not `200`/`302`) attempting to add/view/update tenant-1's catalog task, while the real tenant-1 admin gets `200` on the same reads (proving the fix is tenant-scoped, not globally broken); confirmed all 7 tables have zero `NULL` `tenant_id` rows via direct `psql` queries; confirmed the real webhook delivery fix (above); confirmed `/analytics/overdue` and `/cmdb/export.csv` both still `200` with correct content; confirmed `/`, `/cmdb`, `/tickets/incident` all still `200` for the original tenant-1 admin as a general regression check. All verification test data (tenant 2 and its admin deactivated, not hard-deleted -- blocked by an audit-log FK, consistent with this repo's tombstone convention; tenant-1 test support group/RITM/task hard-deleted since they were newly created for this check alone) removed afterward. |

Also addressed the screenshot's secondary "ugly" complaint on the same panel: `templates/rack_elevation_embed.html`'s front/rear SVGs had a hardcoded `width="140"` (fixed, non-responsive) floating with no visual boundary; changed to `width:100%;height:auto` (matching the pattern the full rack view already used) inside a bordered card (`border:1px solid var(--line)`, padding, rounded corners) so it reads as a normal panel instead of two bare diagrams. No schema/migration change -- JS/template only. Version bumped to `1.50.1` (patch: bug fix, no new capability). Evidence: full suite unchanged at `372 passed, 1 skipped` (this class of bug -- iframe link target behavior -- has no automated coverage in this repo, consistent with `cmdb-topology.js`/`rack-elevation.js` having none; caught and verified by real headless-browser interaction instead, per the [[B-297]] lesson). Deployed live to the real Compose stack (`serviceops-app:1.50.1`, not a throwaway) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head unchanged at `20260807_0065` (no migration this pass). | B-302 | P2 | Verified | Five reported issues from live use, all fixed in one batch. (1) "Recovery set STALE / MISSING why?": root-caused as correct-but-unexplained behavior, not a bug -- the RPO/backup-freshness check has no backing scheduled job in this environment, only the external `tools/record_backup_status.py` CLI, so an install with no real backup pipeline honestly shows unhealthy. Deduplicated the freshness computation (previously independently reimplemented in both `/metrics` and `system_health()`, risking drift) into one shared `_recovery_set_status()` helper in `app.py`; split the collapsed "STALE / MISSING" label into distinct "Missing" (no backup ever recorded) vs. "Stale" (recorded but past RPO) states, and added a new `id="recovery-set"` explanatory panel on the health page naming the RPO window and the CLI tool to resolve it, rather than leaving an unexplained red label. (2) Performance graphs "doesn't make any sense": the charts fetched a per-point `at` timestamp from the payload but never rendered it -- `static/performance-chart.js`'s `renderChart()` now takes a `times` array, adds a `formatTime` helper, per-point hover `<circle>`+`<title>` tooltips, and three time-axis `<text>` labels (first/middle/last point) via a new `.performance-chart-axis-label` class in `static/admin-workspace.css`. (3) Health page's big stat tiles weren't clickable: converted all 7 tiles from `<div>` to `<a>`, reusing the exact clickable-tile pattern already present in `dashboard.html`/`cmdb.html` (`.stats>*` CSS already styled both element types identically, just never used on this page) -- each now links to its real detail destination (`/health`, `/ready`, `/work/open`, or an in-page anchor for performance/errors/active-users/recovery-set). (4) Client Management "Support overview" page showed a ticket-filter "Views" side menu that didn't belong there (it's a filter aside meant for the tickets list, not an overview page, and per the user's report was visually misplaced): removed the `client-shell`/`_client_views.html` wrapper from `templates/client_management_home.html` entirely so it now renders full-width like `client_organizations.html`/`client_contacts.html` already do; deliberately left `templates/client_tickets.html` untouched, since the Views filter genuinely belongs on the page that actually filters tickets by it -- confirmed live that page still shows it correctly. (5) Roles & Permissions had no edit capability: added a new `RolePolicyOverride` model/table (migration `20260807_0065`, plain new-table creation -- deliberately avoided combining `add_column` with `create_index`/`create_foreign_key` in one Alembic batch pass, the exact SQLite batch-mode failure mode hit and fixed in [[B-299]]) as a per-(tenant, role, action) additive layer on top of the Git-backed `config/authorization.json` baseline, mirroring `CiClassPermission`'s "absence of a row = default" storage convention exactly -- "reset to recommended" is simply deleting a role's override rows, since the JSON file itself is already the recommended baseline, with no separate snapshot table needed. New `effective_role_has_action()` wrapper in `app.py` (deliberately not added to `serviceops_core/security.py`, which is documented as intentionally DB-free/Git-backed-only, the same reasoning that already keeps `ci_class_policy.py` a sibling of `security.py` rather than inside it) checks the override table first, falling back to the existing `role_has_action()`; all 12 call sites were switched to it via a scripted find/replace, confirmed with `grep` that no raw `role_has_action(` call remained outside the wrapper itself. `/admin/roles` is now `GET`/`POST`: a checkbox grid for the four non-superadmin roles (superadmin stays fixed/always-implicitly-granted, unchanged from today) plus a per-role "Reset to recommended" button, disabled when that role has no active overrides. This design deliberately never touches `load_policy()`'s `@lru_cache`d JSON (a real cross-worker cache-staleness risk if the file itself were ever mutated at runtime) -- the baseline stays static and cached, only the new DB overlay is checked fresh every request. No schema change beyond the new table; superadmin remains non-overridable by design. Version bumped to `1.50.0` (minor: new edit/drill-down capability, not just fixes). Evidence: full suite `372 passed, 1 skipped` (up from `369/1`) -- new coverage for override save/reset/tenant-isolation on `/admin/roles`. Migration `20260807_0065` verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against a real throwaway Postgres 16 container: `role_policy_override` table and all 7 columns present at head, cleanly absent after downgrade, correctly restored after re-upgrade. Deployed live to the real Compose stack (`serviceops-app:1.50.0`, not a throwaway) -- `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head `20260807_0065` confirmed both via the `/ready` JSON and a direct `alembic_version` query inside the running app container. Verified end-to-end against the live deployment via real HTTP sessions and a real Playwright/Chromium pass (per the [[B-297]] lesson that server-side/HTTP-only checks can miss real bugs): confirmed the recovery-set tile links to `#recovery-set` with the new panel present and correctly worded; confirmed 9 real time-axis labels and 525 real hover-point elements rendered client-side from actual `/admin/system-health/performance.json` data; confirmed all 7 stat tiles are real `<a href>` links resolving `200`; confirmed `/client-management` no longer contains `client-shell`/`client-views` markup while `/client-management/tickets` still does (regression check); confirmed a real CSRF-tokened POST to `/admin/roles` toggling off the agent role's `report` action persisted on re-GET, and that `action=reset&role=agent` restored it to baseline-checked with zero leftover `RolePolicyOverride` rows afterward (checked directly in the database). Zero browser console errors on either the health page or `/admin/roles`; zero bugs found during verification. Not done this pass: no automated test added for the performance-chart JS itself (matches this repo's existing convention for hand-rolled SVG/JS -- `cmdb-topology.js`/`rack-elevation.js` have none either -- verified by browser pass instead). |

| B-300 | P3 | Verified | Immediate follow-up to [[B-299]]: user asked that "each host should be clickable in cmdb, and when you click if it has some rack information, it should show." | B-299 | P2 | Verified | User asked for a GLPI-style physical rack visualization for CMDB servers (shared a real GLPI/Verdanadesk screenshot: front/back elevation, a power-unit/PDU sidebar, space/weight/power stats), with rack and position data "added in a standard way from NetBox." Clarifying questions confirmed full GLPI-shaped parity (not a front-only cut-down version), sourced from **both** an extended NetBox sync **and** manual admin entry (for sites without NetBox), verified through this repo's standard discipline rather than a separate written test-plan document. Planned via `EnterPlanMode` with an `Explore` research pass first, which found ServiceOps had zero rack/position modeling at all: `serviceops_core/netbox_sync.py` read NetBox's `rack.name`/`position` off each device but only ever stashed them as free-text `attributes["NetBox: Rack"]`/`["NetBox: Position"]` strings -- never a real, queryable relationship, no device height/face, and no call to NetBox's own `/api/dcim/racks/` endpoint (needed for each rack's total U-height) anywhere in the file. Every synced device was also hardcoded to `ci_class="Server"` regardless of its real NetBox role, which mattered here because a PDU sidebar needs *something* to ever come back classified as a PDU. New `Rack` model (`serviceops_models.py`) and four new nullable `ConfigurationItem` columns (`rack_id`, `rack_position`, `rack_u_height`, `rack_face`) -- migration `20260807_0064`. `netbox_sync.py` extended: new `/api/dcim/racks/` fetch (a genuinely new API call, not just previously-discarded data) with the same three-tier upsert matching already used for CIs; devices now also read `face` and `device_type.u_height` (both already present in the existing `/api/dcim/devices/` response, just never extracted before); a device's NetBox `role.name` now becomes its `ci_class` directly instead of the old hardcoded "Server," falling back to "Server" only when NetBox has no role set -- disclosed as a real behavior change for existing NetBox-synced CIs (their class will update to something more accurate on next resync). `cmdb_import.py`'s `NETBOX_OWNED_FIELDS` extended to match so CSV import continues to never fight NetBox for the same columns. New `/cmdb/racks` (list + create), `/cmdb/racks/<id>/edit` (admin-gated, blocks deletion while any CI is still mounted, mirroring this codebase's existing detach-before-delete convention rather than orphaning or cascading), and `/cmdb/racks/<id>` (the actual elevation view: front/rear SVG panels with numbered U-slots, a PDU sidebar listing `ci_class="PDU"` CIs, and a stats panel -- space used is a real computed percentage, weight/power are deliberately opportunistic, read only from `attributes.weight_kg`/`attributes.power_watts` when present and degrading to "Not tracked" otherwise, since this app has no dedicated power/weight schema and fabricating a number would be worse than admitting the gap). Manual placement also added directly to the CI edit form (rack/position/height/face fields) for sites without NetBox, satisfying the "both" requirement. Rendering follows `/cmdb/topology`'s established pattern exactly: server builds a plain JSON payload, the template embeds it via a `data-*` attribute (never inline `<script>`, keeps this app's `script-src 'self'` CSP intact), and a new external `static/rack-elevation.js` does all the hand-rolled SVG drawing, no external charting library. Found and fixed two real bugs during verification rather than after shipping: (1) the migration's `ADD COLUMN` + `CREATE INDEX`/`FOREIGN KEY` combination broke SQLite's batch-mode table recreate in a way no existing migration in this repo had ever hit -- reproduced on both a fresh install and a downgrade/re-upgrade cycle, root-caused to the recreate losing track of a column added moments earlier in the same or an immediately prior batch pass; worked around by splitting the FK/index into their own separate batch pass and skipping it on SQLite specifically (test-harness-only in this codebase; PostgreSQL, the only supported production database, gets both the FK and the index, verified directly against a real throwaway Postgres 16 container across fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade). (2) A real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) screenshot pass -- following the lesson from [[B-297]] that server-side/HTTP-only verification can miss real client-side bugs -- caught the Weight/Power stat bars always rendering full green regardless of the actual "Not tracked" state, since the fill `<div>` had no default width and was only ever set explicitly in the tracked branch; fixed in both the JS logic and with a defensive CSS default so the same class of omission can't silently recur. No live NetBox test instance was available in this environment, so NetBox-sourced racks/placement are verified only via the existing mocked-sync test suite (disclosed, not silently skipped); manual placement -- the "admin enters it by hand" half of the "both" requirement -- was verified for real end-to-end against the live deployment: created a real rack, placed three real CIs in it (a Switch, a Server, and a PDU) via the live CI edit form, confirmed all three rendered at the correct U-position/height/face in the real elevation view with zero console errors, confirmed the PDU sidebar and space-used stat reflected them correctly, and confirmed click-through from a device block to its CI edit page worked. While verifying, found two pre-existing, unrelated stray `ConfigurationItem` rows (old stress-test artifacts from [[B-285]]) that had somehow already acquired a `rack_id`/`rack_position` pointing at the newly-created verification rack, with no corresponding audit-log entry and no application code path (confirmed via grep) that could have set them -- cleared their rack fields back to null as part of cleanup since leaving stale/unexplained placement data behind would be misleading regardless of its origin, but the root cause of how those two rows acquired that data remains unexplained and is flagged here rather than silently fixed and forgotten. Version bumped to `1.47.0` (minor: new capability). Evidence: full suite `365 passed, 1 skipped` (up from `358/1`) -- new coverage includes NetBox rack fetch/upsert and three-tier matching, device `face`/`u_height`/`rack_id` resolution, role-based `ci_class` inference (including the no-role-set fallback), rack CRUD route gating and delete-blocked-while-referenced, the rack elevation route's tenant/CI-class-permission filtering (a CI in a class the viewer can't read doesn't appear in the rack view either), and manual placement fields round-tripping through the CI form. Migration `20260807_0064` verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against real Postgres 16, confirming the `rack` table, all four `configuration_item` columns, the FK constraint, and the index all appear and disappear correctly at each step. Deployed and verified live (`serviceops-app:1.47.0`): `/health`/`/ready` green throughout, migration head correctly at `20260807_0064` -- all verification data (the three CIs, the rack, and the two stray rows' rack fields) removed/cleared afterward. |

| B-297 | P1 | Verified | User reported [[B-296]]'s favorites/recently-viewed fix "doesn't work properly" and asked for real UI testing, not just claims. Correctly called out a real gap: [[B-296]]'s verification only exercised `/ui/favorite`/`/ui/history` over plain HTTP and asserted on the server-rendered DOM -- it never actually ran the click handler's JavaScript in a browser, so it missed a real bug entirely. Root cause: the favorite-star click handler is `async` and `await`s `fetch("/ui/favorite")` before touching `event.currentTarget` -- but `event.currentTarget` is a live reference only valid during synchronous event dispatch; the browser resets it to `null` once the handler yields at that first `await`. `event.currentTarget.textContent = ...` therefore threw `TypeError: Cannot set properties of null` and aborted the whole handler immediately, before the favorites list, star icon, or toast were ever updated -- exactly the "click does nothing until refresh" symptom reported, and a class of bug this app's Flask-test-client-based suite structurally cannot catch since it never executes JavaScript. Fixed by capturing `event.currentTarget` into a local variable before the first `await` (`static/platform.js`), the standard fix for this exact JS gotcha. To actually verify this class of fix, installed Playwright + Chromium in this working environment and drove the real login/favorite-toggle/recently-viewed/login-provider-toggle flows in a headless browser against the live deployed app, asserting on zero console/page errors as well as the resulting DOM state -- not just HTTP response bodies. Confirmed: adding and removing a favorite updates the list in place with the correct toast and star-icon state each time; a newly-visited page (`/cmdb/topology`) appears in "Recently viewed" without a reload; the login page's "Forgot your password?" link toggles live as the authentication-provider dropdown is switched between AD and Local. All three passed with zero JS errors on the fixed build; the original bug reproduced cleanly (one `pageerror: Cannot set properties of null`) against the pre-fix build for direct before/after confirmation. Playwright was **not** added as a permanent dependency or test in this repo -- it isn't part of the existing test infrastructure, and adding a new browser-automation toolchain wasn't asked for -- so real-browser JS execution still has no standing regression coverage in this codebase going forward; this specific bug class (correct on the server, correct in a synchronous DOM check, wrong only once real async browser event semantics are involved) is disclosed as a genuine, currently-unclosed gap rather than implied-covered by the passing `pytest` suite. No schema/migration change. Version bumped to `1.46.3` (patch: bug fix). Evidence: full suite still `358 passed, 1 skipped` (unchanged -- this bug was never visible to it). Deployed and verified live (`serviceops-app:1.46.3`): `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head unchanged at `20260806_0063`, then re-ran the same three Playwright scenarios above directly against the final tagged image (not just an intermediate build) to confirm the cache-busted static asset actually shipped the fix -- all passed, zero errors -- then cleaned up all verification data (toggled favorite, recorded history row, temporary `LDAP_ENABLED`/`LDAP_SERVER_URI` settings). Tag hygiene: with `v1.46.1`/`v1.46.2`/`v1.46.3` now occupying three of the last-5 slots, `v1.40.1` and `v1.43.1` fell outside the keep-window (last 5 by semver, union every `.0` release) and were pruned (tag + GitHub release, local and remote) per the standing policy -- the first actual prune since [[B-286]]'s. |
| B-296 | P2 | Verified | Two small UX fixes requested together. (1) "Forgot your password?" on the login page only works for a local account (a local password reset has no effect on an AD/LDAP account), but when both local and AD/LDAP sign-in are enabled, the link was shown unconditionally regardless of which provider was selected in the login form's authentication-source dropdown -- AD is that dropdown's default option, so most affected logins saw a reset link that could never work for them. Fixed by starting the link hidden whenever both providers are enabled and toggling it live via a `change` listener on the provider `<select>` (`static/platform.js`), rather than deciding it once at page render; unaffected when only one provider is configured (link never shows for AD-only, always shows for local-only, unchanged from before). (2) "Set as favorite" and "Recently viewed" in the top nav were both purely server-rendered once per page load with no client-side follow-up: clicking "Add to favorites" updated the star icon and button label but never touched the favorites list underneath, and "Recently viewed" never showed the page you were currently on until you navigated away and back -- both required a manual refresh to see the actual result, which read as the button "not responding." Root cause was that `/ui/favorite` and `/ui/history` only ever returned a bare acknowledgment (`{"active": ...}` or an empty `204`), giving the client nothing to render the new list entry from. Both routes now also return the affected `url`/`label` in their JSON response, added to `config/field_projections.json`'s `ui_action_ack` governed projection (the same allowed-fields mechanism already used for every other UI acknowledgment in this app, not a new one-off); `platform.js` uses that to prepend/remove the corresponding link in the favorites/history `<div data-favorites-list>`/`<div data-history-list>` containers in place, with a brief fade/slide-in transition on the new entry (a plain CSS `transition`, automatically skipped under the app's existing `.reduced-motion` preference class -- no separate JS branch needed). No schema/migration change. Version bumped to `1.46.2` (patch: UX fix, no new capability). Evidence: full suite `358 passed, 1 skipped` (up from `357/1`) -- new tests cover `/ui/favorite`'s and `/ui/history`'s JSON now including `url`/`label`, and the login page rendering `data-forgot-password-link hidden` when LDAP is enabled alongside local auth. Deployed and verified live (`serviceops-app:1.46.2`): `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head unchanged at `20260806_0063`. Real HTTP-session verification: toggled a real favorite on/off and recorded a real history entry, confirmed both `/ui/favorite` and `/ui/history` responses carried the expected `url`/`label` and the subsequently-rendered page included the new list entries; temporarily enabled `LDAP_ENABLED`/`LDAP_SERVER_URI` directly against the live database and confirmed the login page rendered the provider dropdown with the forgot-password link starting hidden, then removed those settings and confirmed the link reverts to always-visible in local-only mode -- all verification data (the toggled favorite, the recorded history row, the temporary LDAP settings) removed afterward. Not independently verified: actual pointer/`change`-event behavior in a real browser (verified via direct HTTP/DOM-content assertions instead, consistent with this environment's lack of a browser automation tool) -- the JS logic itself is a small, direct DOM patch with no framework indirection, so this is a low-risk gap, but disclosed rather than silently claimed as browser-tested. |
| B-295 | P2 | Verified | User had made LDAP fixes on a separate office machine checkout (`dmiltraining85`) they couldn't push from directly, and shared screenshots of the diff/commit ("LDAP optimized") asking that it be replicated here so it could be pushed to GitHub as usual. Flagged the risk up front -- transcribing multi-file code diffs from screenshots (some cut off mid-function) carries real transcription risk, and offered safer transfer alternatives (`git format-patch`, a reachable remote/bundle); user explicitly chose to proceed with screenshot reconstruction anyway. Reconstructed against this repo's actual current source (confirmed every "before" context line in the screenshots matched byte-for-byte before applying each hunk, rather than pasting blind) rather than trusting the image transcription alone, and ran the full verification pass this repo requires for any change, not just a syntax check. Root problem being fixed: `sync_directory()` (`serviceops_core/ldap_sync.py`) previously crashed/mishandled the case where an AD `manager` attribute points a user at their own DN (hit while investigating a real user's org-chart data) -- now detects the self-reference, skips only that one manager assignment, and reports it by username via new `self_manager_skipped`/`self_manager_users` summary fields, surfaced in the admin sync-results table and flash message (`templates/itil_admin.html`). The full-directory search moved from a single `service.search()` call to ldap3's `paged_search` (500-entry pages), which doesn't silently cap results the way an unpaged AD query can on a large directory. Restored `sync_ldap_manager_on_login()` (new function, `app.py`) so a user's `manager_id` resolves on their very first LDAP login rather than only after an admin next runs a directory sync -- provisions a normal LDAP-identity account for a never-logged-in manager exactly like `sync_directory()`'s existing manager-provisioning path, is itself self-manager-safe, and never raises into the login flow on any lookup failure (best-effort, matching this module's existing "a DNS/directory miss is not an error" convention elsewhere in the codebase). `ldap_authenticate()` now builds its LDAP attribute list from the configured `LDAP_ATTR_MAP` instead of a fixed set, and passes `profile_attrs` through to `provision_external_user()` so profile fields (title/department/etc.) populate on first login, not only via a later directory sync. Found and fixed one real regression introduced by the reconstruction itself: switching to `paged_search` initially made a directory search that legitimately returns zero entries indistinguishable from a failed search (both hit the same "if not entries: error" branch) -- would have made a real empty-but-successful scheduled sync report as failed. Caught immediately by the existing `test_ldap_sync_schedule.py` suite (not something that had to be guessed at), fixed by removing that check since `paged_search` raises on genuine failure rather than returning a boolean the way the old `.search()` call did, and that exception path is already caught per-tenant by `process_ldap_sync_schedule`. Also updated both `test_ldap_sync.py`'s and `test_ldap_sync_schedule.py`'s `FakeConnection` mocks to support `extend.standard.paged_search` -- neither mock's required change was visible in the source screenshots at all, but both were necessary for the existing mocked-LDAP test suite to keep passing against the new search API; without this every existing `sync_directory` test would have broken. No schema/migration change. Version bumped to `1.46.1` (patch: behavior fix to existing LDAP sync/login, no new capability) -- the screenshots referenced the office machine reaching `1.46.8` through its own separate sequence of local bumps, but only one squashed commit's diff was actually provided, so no intermediate version history was fabricated to match that number; this repo's own lineage continues cleanly from `1.46.0`. Evidence: full suite `357 passed, 1 skipped` (up from `356/1`) -- the new `test_self_manager_dn_is_skipped_and_reported` plus all 9 pre-existing `ldap_sync` tests and both `ldap_sync_schedule` tests passing again after the mock/regression fixes above. Deployed and verified live (`serviceops-app:1.46.1`): `/health`/`/ready` green, migration head correctly unchanged at `20260806_0063`, confirmed via a real authenticated session that `/itil/administration` renders the updated "Scheduled sync is enabled/disabled..." copy with no template error, and confirmed clean app/worker logs post-deploy. Not verified, disclosed rather than silently skipped: no live AD/LDAP server was available in this environment to exercise a real login-time self-manager scenario end-to-end the way the office machine's investigation against `ganesha.palle`'s real directory data reportedly did -- coverage here rests on the full mocked-LDAP test suite plus the live deploy's health/render checks, not a real directory round-trip. |
| B-294 | P1 | Verified | User asked to extend ServiceOps beyond internal ITIL tickets with a Zendesk-style external client/customer support tool, as its own switchable section gated to the "sysops team" -- referencing a screenshot of Zendesk's agent ticket queue (views sidebar, ticket-status pills, assignee grouping) as the target shape. Found this already substantially built as uncommitted work in the working tree at session start (models, migration, routes, templates, nav wiring, and tests already present) rather than something to build from scratch -- per this repo's engineering-workflow discipline, picked up and completed that in-progress work rather than redoing or discarding it. New `ClientOrganization`/`ClientContact`/`ClientTicket`/`ClientTicketMessage` models (serviceops_models.py) and migration `20260806_0063`: a `client_ticket` (CXT-numbered) belongs to a `client_contact` (external identity, not an application login) which belongs to a `client_organization`, is owned by the tenant's "SysOps" support group, and carries a thread of `client_ticket_message` rows with `visibility` public/internal (public replies vs. internal notes, matching Zendesk's own reply/note split) and an `event_type` for system-generated entries like status-change audit lines. Every tenant is backfilled a governed "SysOps" / `group_type="Client Support"` support group by the migration (idempotent via `NOT EXISTS`, safe to re-run); membership is never auto-inferred, an administrator must explicitly add agents/managers to it. New `GET /client-management` hub (mine/unassigned/unsolved/pending/recent/solved counts), `/client-management/tickets` (filterable views + free-text search across ticket number/subject/contact name/email/organization name), `/client-management/tickets/new` and `/client-management/tickets/<id>` (reply, internal note, status/priority/type/assignee update -- a status change auto-appends an internal audit message), and `/client-management/organizations` / `/client-management/contacts` CRUD, all behind `require_client_management` / `user_can_access_client_management()`: admin/superadmin always allowed, otherwise only members or the manager of the tenant's SysOps group -- exactly the "switchable if you have the correct access level" scoping asked for. A new sidebar nav group only renders at all (`client_management_access` context flag) for users who pass that check, with an open-ticket-count badge, so the feature is fully invisible rather than visible-but-403 to anyone without access. Tenant-isolated throughout (verified: a client ticket in tenant 2 returns 404 for a tenant-1 SysOps agent, not a cross-tenant leak). Found and fixed two real bugs during this session's live-Postgres verification pass that the inherited work's SQLite-backed test suite had not caught, illustrating exactly why this repo's discipline requires real Postgres verification and not just the automated suite: (1) migration `20260806_0063`'s four `op.create_table` calls were unguarded, but `20260726_0001` (the baseline migration) creates every table present in the *current* ORM metadata on a fresh database via `db.metadata.create_all` -- since `ClientOrganization`/etc. were already defined in `serviceops_models.py` by the time that baseline migration runs, a fresh database already has these tables before `20260806_0063` executes, and the unguarded `create_table` calls then fail with "table already exists." Every other post-baseline migration that adds a new table already guards with `sa.inspect(bind).has_table(...)` for this exact reason (e.g. `20260806_0060`, `20260805_0058`/`0059`) -- this one didn't; fixed the same way, while leaving the SysOps backfill `INSERT` unguarded and always-run since it's independently idempotent via `NOT EXISTS` and is the only path that backfills SysOps for tenants that predate this feature. (2) `client_contacts()` and `client_ticket_new()` filtered `ClientOrganization`/`ClientContact` by `id=request.form.get("organization_id"/"contact_id")` -- a raw string. SQLite compares loosely and silently coerced it, so every automated test passed; PostgreSQL's strict typing rejects `integer = character varying` outright, producing a real 500 on the very first live "create contact" attempt. Fixed with `request.form.get(..., type=int)` at both call sites, matching this codebase's existing `int(request.form["..."])` convention elsewhere. Version bumped to `1.46.0` (minor: new capability); this release also carries the persistent-accordion sidebar navigation redesign documented separately in [[B-293]], which was mid-flight in the same working tree and shares this version number. Evidence: full suite `356 passed, 1 skipped` (up from `354/1` before this feature's 6 pre-existing tests plus fixes); migration `20260806_0063` verified with a full fresh-upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade cycle against a real throwaway PostgreSQL 16 container (confirmed all four tables present after upgrade, absent after downgrade, present again after re-upgrade, and the SysOps backfill row present at every upgraded state). Deployed and verified live (`serviceops-app:1.46.0`) against the real running deployment, not a throwaway stack: created a real agent user, added it to the real SysOps group via direct group membership, and through real authenticated HTTP sessions -- confirmed a non-SysOps agent gets `403` on `/client-management` while admin and the SysOps-granted agent get `200`; created a real `ClientOrganization`, `ClientContact`, and `ClientTicket` end-to-end through the live UI forms (this is what caught bug (2) above, live, before it reached the user); posted a public reply and an internal note, updated status/priority, confirmed both the ticket list search and the ticket detail page reflected the changes; confirmed `/health`/`/ready` stayed green throughout with the correct migration head `20260806_0063` -- then deleted all verification data (messages, ticket, contact, organization, group membership) and tombstoned both temporary agent accounts (renamed/deactivated, never hard-deleted, per the established `Audit` FK/append-only-trigger convention) from the live database. Not done this pass: no client-facing self-service portal (agent-side workspace only, matching the screenshot's agent queue view, not a customer-login portal); no email-to-ticket ingestion for the "Email" channel (channel is recorded but there's no inbound mail connector yet); no SLA policy application to client tickets (they use the same status/priority vocabulary as internal tickets but aren't wired into the existing `SLADefinition`/business-calendar engine). |

| B-052 | P1 | In progress | Release 1.39.0 adds S3-compatible attachment upload/download and readiness validation with local-volume fallback; existing type verification, SHA-256 and optional ClamAV remain enforced before upload. Representative provider-side encryption, retention/lifecycle, failure and migration testing remain. Third and final item in this session's "push self-runnable evidence on In-progress items" sweep after [[B-071]]/[[B-070]], continuing until the day's session limit. Real rehearsal: a disposable MinIO (genuinely S3-compatible, not a mock) instance plus a fully isolated ServiceOps app/worker/db instance wired to it, both on a separate Docker network from the live shared deployment (same isolation discipline as [[B-071]]/[[B-070]] -- confirmed undisturbed and healthy throughout). **Real byte-perfect roundtrip confirmed first**: uploaded a real file to a real ticket, confirmed the object actually landed in the MinIO bucket under the expected key (not the local-volume fallback path -- explicitly checked the container's local uploads directory was empty), SHA-256 matched the database record exactly, and download returned identical bytes. **Real failure injection then found three real crash sites**, all sharing one root cause (an unguarded boto3/S3 client call): `save_ticket_attachment()`, `save_email_attachment()` (the [[B-260]]/email-channel inbound path), and `attachment_download()` all had zero error handling around their S3 calls, so a real MinIO outage crashed all three into generic 500s. The two upload paths had a second, worse bug on top: the local temp file's cleanup line was only reachable on the success path, so *every* failed upload leaked a temp file on local disk permanently -- confirmed directly (stopped MinIO, uploaded, found the orphaned file sitting in the container's uploads directory). Fixed all three: both upload paths now catch the failure, unconditionally remove the local temp file, and return a clean user-facing error (a flashed message for the ticket path, `None` for the email-ingest path matching its existing malware-reject return shape) instead of propagating an unhandled exception; the download path now returns a clean `503` instead of a `500`. Confirmed all three fixes against a *second* real MinIO outage on the disposable stack (upload: `302` with the flash message, zero orphaned temp files this time; download: `503`) and confirmed clean recovery after restarting MinIO (both paths back to normal, byte-perfect download). Two new regression tests (`test_ticket_attachment_upload_degrades_gracefully_on_object_storage_outage`, `test_attachment_download_degrades_gracefully_on_object_storage_outage`, monkeypatched broken S3 client, no real MinIO needed for the automated suite) -- this whole code path had *zero* test coverage before this pass, confirmed by grepping the test suite for any `object_storage`/`OBJECT_STORAGE` reference and finding none. **Two more gaps confirmed precisely, not assumed, and left open**: direct MinIO inspection (`mc encrypt info`, `mc ilm rule ls`) confirmed the app configures no server-side encryption and no lifecycle policy on the bucket -- both are entirely deployment/infra-owned by design (the app doesn't request per-object SSE on any upload call either), a reasonable division of responsibility but worth stating precisely rather than assuming; and no `tools/` script exists to migrate existing local-volume attachments to S3 for a deployment switching backends after going live -- a real, disclosed gap, not attempted this pass. Version bumped to `1.62.2` (patch: bug fix). Evidence: full suite `444 passed, 2 skipped` (up from `442/2`). No schema change; migration head unchanged at `20260811_0077`. Deployed live (`serviceops-app:1.62.2`) -- `/health`/`/ready` green. Still genuinely remaining: representative provider-side encryption/retention/lifecycle testing against a real cloud provider (not just confirming the app's own scope boundary against MinIO), and a supported local-to-S3 migration path. **Local-to-S3 migration gap, now closed**: new `tools/migrate_local_attachments_to_object_storage.py` (idempotent -- verifies SHA-256 by downloading and re-hashing before removing the local copy, never trusting the upload call's own success; leaves both the local file and the database row untouched on any failure, so a partial run is always safe to re-run). Verified end-to-end against real infrastructure on a disposable instance: real local attachments migrated to a real MinIO bucket, confirmed objects landed correctly and local files were removed, then the app was switched to `OBJECT_STORAGE_*` mode and a byte-identical download was confirmed through the real `attachment_download()` route afterward. The tool's own new regression tests (`tests/test_migrate_local_attachments.py`) caught a real bug before shipping: it read `UPLOAD_FOLDER` from the OS environment instead of Flask's `app.config`, unlike every other attachment code path in this app -- fixed to use `current_app.config["UPLOAD_FOLDER"]`. Version bumped to `1.62.4` (patch, since only a standalone tool + tests changed, no app.py/runtime behavior). Representative encryption/retention testing against a real (non-MinIO) cloud provider remains, since that needs real cloud credentials this environment doesn't have. |
